- categories: eess.IV, cs.AI, cs.CV
- published: 2026-11-25T11:52:41Z
- authors: Chibike Ugbam, Muhammad Bilal, Jia Wu, Shafaq Murad, Rizwan Qureshi, Hazrat Ali
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34230

Deep learning models for prostate MRI segmentation often struggle to generalize when trained on limited heterogeneous data, leading to boundary errors and over-segmentation. We present an augmentation framework using mask-conditioned synthetic and diverse T2-weighted MRI volumes to improve boundary precision and reduce over-segmentation. We employ a 2.5D weakly mask-conditioned denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM) via channel concatenation. The diffusion model conditions on stacks of adjacent binary masks from training folds. The model generates T2 volumes consistent with the conditioning anatomy while diversifying tissue appearance. We evaluate the proposed approach on two cohorts of data: (i) the LUND probe cohort (n=432) for strategy selection and training nnU-Net from scratch with a 23% synthetic data mix, and (ii) the Medical Segmentation Decathlon (MSD) prostate cohort (n=32) evaluated with a 20% synthetic data mix. For the LUND cohort, we found that training an nnU-Net model from scratch with a 23% synthetic data mix reduced the mean false-positive voxels per case from 11,102 (baseline) to 10,757. On the MSD cohort, the augmented model with a 20% synthetic mix achieved a mean cross-validated Dice of 0.9094, improving over the baseline (0.8935) by 1.59 percentage points and reducing mean false-positive voxels by 23.1% (4,201 to 3,229). Experimental results on both these cohorts suggest that mask-conditioned DDPM augmentation can reduce over-segmentation errors and improve boundary adherence in data-constrained settings.


### arXiv 2611.34222 - The Socratic PAUSE: safeguarding scientific judgement at AI speed

- categories: cs.CY, cs.AI, cs.HC, cs.LG
- published: 2026-11-25T11:47:46Z
- authors: Abdulkadir Celik, Sir Bashir M. Al-Hashimi
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34222

Generative AI can turn a tentative scientific question into hypotheses, methods, code and interpretations within minutes. Yet generating a contribution does not establish that it deserves scientific reliance: researchers must still examine its evidence, assumptions and implications, and remain accountable for the decision to rely on it. Using Einstein's sketch of scientific discovery as a lens, we argue that AI has accelerated discovery far more than justification. In human-AI research partnerships, confidence can therefore outgrow evidence through three mechanisms: recursive reinforcement, loss of provenance and miscalibrated reliance on AI-generated contributions. Verification debt accumulates when such contributions are carried into later claims or decisions without adequate scrutiny. Accordingly, we propose the Socratic Pause as a deliberate checkpoint at consequential decision gates, where an AI contribution remains provisional while a researcher examines its basis. The PAUSE protocol structures this examination through five steps: Pin down the claim, Ask and challenge, Uncover assumptions and alternatives, Scrutinise the evidence and Exercise judgement. It concludes with a recorded decision to accept, revise, reject or defer the contribution. We show how AI research tools can support this practice without displacing human judgement, and outline a framework for evaluating whether it improves verification, decision-making and independent understanding. The aim is to preserve the freedom to explore with AI while ensuring that those responsible for the work can say what was examined, what remains uncertain and why the next step is justified.


### arXiv 2611.34218 - GeoCAM: Self-Supervised Sonar-Optical Representation Learning via Geographic Overlap Supervision

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T11:42:38Z
- authors: Fadi Abdeladhim Zidi, Andrea De Salve, Giuseppe Celeste, Abdellah Zakaria Sellam, Paolo Spagnolo, Cosimo Distante
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34218

Cross-modal self-supervised learning for paired side-scan sonar and underwater optical imagery is a promising direction for benthic habitat understanding, but it remains underexplored because existing methods do not fully exploit the one-to-many correspondences and geographic metadata available in real opti-acoustic surveys. This limits the ability to learn representations that are both semantically meaningful and geometrically consistent from abundant unlabeled marine data. To address this problem, we present GeoCAM, a geography-guided framework that learns a shared embedding space for sonar tiles and their associated optical views without semantic annotations. GeoCAM achieves its goal by combining three components: (i) a Set Attention Aggregator that adaptively summarizes multiple optical observations into a single tile-level representation; (ii) a geometry-weighted InfoNCE objective that converts GPS-derived footprint overlap into soft positive supervision; and (iii) an optional cross-modal masked reconstruction branch that encourages physically grounded cross-modal alignment. Experiments on the BenthiCat SSS-CAM dataset show that GeoCAM can be effectively exploited to learn which sonar and camera samples describe the same seabed patch, improving cross-modal retrieval and alignment over strong contrastive baselines. Code is available at https://github.com/FadiZidiDz/GeoCAM.


### arXiv 2611.34215 - PROVE: Mitigating Long-Horizon LLM Collusion with Reinforcement Learning from Verified Evidence

- categories: cs.AI, cs.MA
- published: 2026-11-25T11:39:39Z
- authors: Haochen Luo, Jie Fu, Mukai Li, Yuqi Wang, Qi Liu
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34215

When LLM agents verify each other's work over a long interaction, they drift into collusion: they come to accept work without the evidence that the verification protocol requires. Collusion is absent at the start and builds up episode by episode, which we trace to accurate feedback accumulating in memory. An agent that follows the rule is told that its verdict was wrong whenever its peer was right, so breaking the rule is the reward-maximizing choice, and agents talk themselves into it in their private reflections. Existing mitigations leave the agents unchanged; we train the behavior out. PROVE (Protocol Reinforcement from Verified Evidence) is a multi-agent reinforcement learning method that rewards verdicts by the evidence behind them rather than by their outcome, and credits every call an agent makes, including the calls that solve its own task, with the outcome that call controls. On Qwen3.6-27B, a LoRA adapter trained on six-episode interactions takes the collusion rate on held-out task sequences at the original ten-episode horizon from 65.0% to 1.0%, with no detectable change in task success. Collusion that converged in every interaction now converges in none, and the gain holds four episodes beyond the longest interaction seen in training. The trained 27B model colludes less than every model evaluated in the original study, closed-weight frontier models included. Code is available at https://github.com/Haochen-Luo/PROVE.


### arXiv 2611.34207 - Beyond Logits: Coherent Hallucination Mitigation via Attention Contrastive Decoding

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T11:32:00Z
- authors: Yujia Chen, Rui Sun, Huayu Mai, Wangkai Li, Zhangyu He, Bingzhou Wang
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34207

Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) demonstrate impressive multimodal capabilities, yet suffer from hallucination - generating factually inaccurate content. Contrastive Decoding (CD) mitigates this by contrasting amateur and expert branches at the logit level. However, our investigation reveals that such logit-level interventions fundamentally compromise generation coherence, necessitating restrictive penalty constraints unrelated to hallucination suppression. We introduce Attention Contrastive Decoding (ACD), a training-free plug-in that complements logit-level CD by relocating part of the contrastive operations to the attention mechanism. Operating at an earlier stage of the forward pass, ACD performs smooth semantic-preserving interventions through an Adaptive Subtraction Strategy (ASS), which attenuates hallucination-associated attention patterns while amplifying critical visual information. Extensive experiments demonstrate that combining ACD with existing CD methods (e.g., VCD+ACD) produces substantially more coherent outputs with further reduced hallucinations, eliminating restrictive penalties while enabling trustworthy multimodal generation.


### arXiv 2611.34190 - SOLO: Towards Sample-Faithful and Detectable LLM Watermarking under Repeated Sampling via Content-Anchored Single-Owner Contexts

- categories: cs.AI, cs.CL
- published: 2026-11-25T11:19:40Z
- authors: Zhongliang Guo, Yan Lin, Yifei Qian, Shuai Zhao, Luwei Xiao, Daizong Liu
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34190

Providers watermark the outputs of large language models, while applications sample many responses per prompt for self-consistency, best-of-n selection, and uncertainty estimation. We show that deployed configurations of distortion-free watermarks, which deduplicate keyed contexts only within a response, couple the responses of a request: answer templates make all responses query the watermark with the same context before the answer, so collisions across responses, usually assumed rare, become the norm. Key pools reduce or avoid the coupling at the cost of multiple testing, and the exact remedy of a context history shared across the request leaves many positions unwatermarked. We propose SOLO, which keeps a request-scoped context history but anchors its keyed contexts on content selected by min-hash, so that siblings rarely compete for a context and random edits stay local. Its responses are jointly distributed as independent unwatermarked responses, each detectable with a single key. On five language models from three families and three benchmarks, SOLO preserves self-consistency and uncertainty estimation and, at a 1% false positive rate, detects more responses than four-token key pools on most tasks, also after randomly editing 10% of the tokens, and more than the context history on most clean tasks. Code is available at https://github.com/ZhongliangGuo/solo-watermark.


### arXiv 2611.34188 - MINE: A Training-Free Defense against Indirect Prompt Injection via Mutual Information and Embedding Variance

- categories: cs.CL, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T11:16:43Z
- authors: Truong Phan, Ngoc-Quan Pham, Dat Quoc Nguyen
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34188

Existing defenses against indirect prompt injection in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) are inherently reactive: prompt-engineering methods are often overridden by the injected instructions, while supervised detectors must be retrained for each new attack style. We propose a different paradigm: defend by maximizing relevance, not by detecting attacks. Our method, MINE, is training-free and attack-agnostic, and it also serves as a query-conditioned reranker. To evaluate defenses under realistic conditions, we introduce LC-Inj-SQuAD, a long-context benchmark with an average of 4,032 words per context. We evaluate on four datasets spanning short-context QA (Inj-SQuAD, Inj-TriviaQA), long-context QA (LC-Inj-SQuAD), and agent tool-call injection (InjecAgent). Across all four, MINE achieves the best accuracy--security trade-off among training-free defenses. It is also the only defense that stays usable against a fully adaptive attacker, generalizes to agent tool-call injection, and achieves the lowest inference latency on long contexts.


### arXiv 2611.34184 - Hierarchical Semantic Learning for Multimodal Sentiment Analysis with Missing Modalities

- categories: cs.MM, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T11:13:38Z
- authors: Chunlei Meng, Rong Fu, Zhan Chen, Yi Liu, Zhuo Zou, LiDa Xu
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34184

Multimodal sentiment analysis (MSA) in real-world systems must handle unpredictable modality missingness caused by sensor failures, privacy masking, and unstable transmission. Existing methods either learn reliability-aware representations or reconstruct missing inputs, often using a single flattened encoder output. This overlooks deep encoders' semantic hierarchy and intra- and inter-modal interactions, causing semantic averaging and ill-posed mappings. We propose Hierarchical Semantic Learning (HSL), a framework that exploits semantic hierarchy for robust MSA with missing modalities. Hierarchical Semantic Decoupling exposes low-, mid-, and high-level feature streams from each modality encoder. Cross-Modal Cross-Level Semantic Reconstruction performs ladder-style recovery, using high-level semantics from observed modalities as global anchors to progressively reconstruct mid-level structures and low-level details of missing modalities. To stabilize cross-level reconstruction, Hierarchical Cascade Error Supervisor applies prototypical contrastive regularization to observed and reconstructed features, enforcing level-specific semantic consistency and limiting error propagation. Finally, Hierarchical Interactive Fusion captures dense interactions across modalities and semantic levels to produce robust predictions under diverse missingness patterns. Experiments on CMU-MOSI, CMU-MOSEI, IEMOCAP, and CH-SIMS show that HSL outperforms baselines in most missing-modality settings.


### arXiv 2611.34181 - Agentic-Level Adversarial Purification: Revisiting Diffusion Purifiers on Native-Resolution Agentic Inputs

- categories: cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.CV
- published: 2026-11-25T11:10:06Z
- authors: Yan Lin, Zhongliang Guo, Jingyu Lin, Daizong Liu
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34181

Vision-language agents act on native-resolution renders of multi-element pages, where one perturbed photo or a few altered text strokes can redirect the decision. Any content provider can craft such a perturbation on the open model behind the agent, so a plug-in purifier is the natural first line of defense. Yet diffusion purifiers were designed and evaluated at the fixed resolution of the diffusion model on classifier or natural-image benchmarks, and defense evaluations for agents test JPEG, blur and noise but no diffusion purifier. We present the first study of adversarial purification at the agentic level, for an agent built on Qwen3-VL-8B: purifiers run at native resolution on multi-element inputs, face grey-box attackers who know the agent's model but not the defense, and are compared at matched clean cost. We show that treating agentic inputs as classifier inputs misleads both evaluation and design. Resizing the input to the resolution of the diffusion model, as existing purifiers do, raises the TextVQA clean cost of the Divide-and-Conquer purifier (DC) from 4.0 to 35.7 points. On web-shop pages, DC keeps 85.0% grey-box robust accuracy against web-hardened perturbations, where cheap input transformations at matched clean cost keep at most 40.0%, and this robustness comes from the global noise level: input-estimated masks and noise levels add none. These findings hold on a disjoint held-out set and on a second VLM and yield three requirements for defenses, we release the protocol.


### arXiv 2611.34169 - Multi-view fnirs identifies neurophysiological subtypes in post-stroke aphasia

- categories: q-bio.NC, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:59:58Z
- authors: Zijian Kang, Bingbing Lin, Jinglei Ni, Meifang Tian, Wai Ting Siok, Jia Huang
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34169

Post-stroke aphasia is clinically heterogeneous, and conventional classifications may not capture differences in brain functional organisation. We tested whether resting-state and task-based functional near-infrared spectroscopy (fNIRS) could identify neurophysiological subgroups alongside clinical assessment.Seventy-nine patients underwent unsupervised clustering using three fNIRS feature views: resting-state activity and connectivity, language-task activation, and rest-to-task connectivity reconfiguration. Similarity network fusion and spectral clustering integrated the views. Clinical and behavioural measures were excluded from clustering.Three clusters (n = 32, 17 and 30) were identified. This solution had the largest eigengap and was the only candidate significant after permutation testing and Holm correction (adjusted P = 0.001). It was stable under subsampling (mean adjusted Rand index, 0.773), but agreement fell to 0.217 when the resting-state view was removed. Clusters differed in most regional intrinsic-activity measures and all resting-state connectivity summaries, with few differences in task activation or connectivity reconfiguration. Their profiles showed higher right anterior prefrontal activity, distributed fronto-sensorimotor and parietal alterations, and reduced interhemispheric connectivity, respectively.Cluster membership was not significantly associated with conventional aphasia subtype (Cramér's V = 0.287), and baseline clinical language scores and task performance did not differ between clusters.These findings identify three neurophysiological subgroups distinguished mainly by intrinsic activity and resting-state functional connectivity, not clearly aligned with conventional aphasia classifications or baseline clinical measures. Multi-view fNIRS analysis may complement clinical characterisation, requiring independent and longitudinal validation.


### arXiv 2611.34158 - Strategic and Robust Task Allocation for Combat Objectives in Adversarial Multi-Agent Environments

- categories: cs.MA, cs.AI, cs.LG
- published: 2026-11-25T10:51:03Z
- authors: Jonas Peché, Dmitry Matveev, Vladimir Ciric, Lizaveta Sakalova, Günter Wallner
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34158

Task allocation in contested-objective team games requires coordinated decisions under uncertainty, spatial constraints, and changing tactical conditions. In such settings, multiple agents must anticipate both teammate behavior and opponent actions. We study a hybrid predict-then-optimize approach for assigning controllable agents to objectives in real time. A masked multi-head model predicts tactical signals for each agent, including survival chance, local superiority, combat interaction, travel time, and objective contribution. These signals are combined with rule-based terms in a score matrix, and a minimum-cost flow solver computes coordinated global assignments constrained by capacity, occupancy, stability, and defensive strategies. Learning is limited to tactical scoring, while explicit rules and solver structure preserve controllability and interpretability. We evaluate the system using predictive metrics, offline assignment diagnostics, online A/B evaluation, and latency measurements. The system is an R\&D research prototype built on a commercial game and is not deployed in the shipped game. The results indicate that learned tactical estimates can support stable, feasible, and inspectable objective allocation under strict real-time constraints.


### arXiv 2611.34152 - Lost in the Grid: Where Multimodal LLMs Fail Between Seeing and Planning

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:46:43Z
- authors: Luoxi Zhang, Tianyi Tan, Jiawei Liu, Xingyu Zeng, Min Yang, Shiwen Ni
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34152

Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) excel at static visual understanding and are increasingly applied to spatial planning, where a model interprets a visual layout and outputs an action sequence to reach a goal. Grid games are a common testbed for this ability because their rules are explicit and a solver can verify every move. Yet existing benchmarks mostly report end-to-end success rates, which hide whether a failure stems from visual perception, rule application, or multi-step planning. We introduce GridProbe, a diagnostic benchmark that 1) locates the failing stage between visual perception and planning and 2) verifies the diagnosis by targeted fine-tuning. On Sokoban, GridProbe pairs end-to-end solving with four probes (recognition, localization, single-step prediction, and state tracking) and gives every board both as an image and as an equivalent text grid. Evaluating seven MLLMs on Sokoban and a maze game reveals distinct bottlenecks behind similar success rates: proprietary models are limited by multi-step planning, whereas open-source models are limited by rule application or by visual grid indexing, i.e., counting rows and columns. Planning failures also hide perception failures: when planning is easier, the visual cost of the same model grows from 7.6 to 37.7 points. The diagnosis identifies what to train: fine-tuning only the vision module of the perception-limited model on board reading raises its interactive Sokoban success 3.5-fold (5.8% to 20.4%) and transfers zero-shot to the unseen maze game (single-turn success 13.8% to 67.1%), whereas the same data does not help a rule-limited model and training on solver solutions brings no gain. We will release the benchmark and code at https://github.com/suat-llm/GridProbe.


### arXiv 2611.34147 - Letting Models Manage Their Own Context: Where We Stand

- categories: cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:44:04Z
- authors: Alessio Devoto, Avinash Vem, Tara Safavi, Sanjay Kariyappa, Yu-Hsuan Tseng, Paul Furgale
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34147

Agent harnesses today manage context on the model's behalf: when the window fills, the harness triggers a compaction that summarizes the history. We study the alternative, \emph{model-driven} context management, in which the model itself decides when and what to compact through a context API. We gave models control over their own context in \nooa, NVIDIA's open source agent harness, and compared the two strategies on document research and machine learning engineering across context budgets from 8K to 512K tokens. Models that use the context API effectively reach higher accuracy, often with fewer input tokens, especially at tight budgets: \textbf{GPT 6.1 nearly triples its score at a 32K budget} on OfficeQA Pro v2 (21\%~$\rightarrow$~56\%), and \textbf{Kimi gains with up to 41\% fewer input tokens}. Models differ widely, however, in how often they use the API, how aggressively they compact, and when they start compacting, and prompt changes shift that timing. Finally, we show that \textbf{inference engines can make model-driven edits cheaper}: by reusing the KV cache of the unchanged suffix after an edit, \textbf{we cut prefill compute by up to 15\% per request with no measurable change in accuracy}. Together, these results point to a new co-design frontier: models trained to manage their own context, served by inference engines that support KV cache editing.


### arXiv 2611.34142 - Reproducer Synthesis for AI-Reported Bugs in the Linux Kernel

- categories: cs.OS, cs.AI, cs.SE
- published: 2026-11-25T10:40:46Z
- authors: Xiao Wu, Yunbo Ni, Hao Sun, Yuanyuan Yuan, Shaohua Li
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34142

Agentic review systems are increasingly flagging suspected bugs in Linux kernel patches as textual reports. A maintainer who receives such reports needs further inspection to determine whether the report is true. The inspection requires understanding whether the bug can be triggered and, if necessary, manually constructing one. As a consequence, despite the excessive textual explanation, many reports go unanswered. In this paper, we argue that a runnable proof-of-concept (PoC) is the key information in such AI-generated kernel review reports. It is, however, challenging to automatically synthesize a valid and runnable PoC. We present ReproCue, a framework that generates PoCs for AI-generated Linux kernel reviews. ReproCue replays the historical kernel bugs and learns one reproduction finite-state-machine (FSM) per subsystem from successful runs. An FSM records the steps a reproduction goes through, together with the requirements observed at each step. Given a new review, ReproCue follows the FSM of that subsystem, adjusting the PoC and the environment as execution shows what is missing. In 100 recent AI-generated reviews, ReproCue writes PoCs for 49 of them, compared with 10 for the current agentic reproducer and 9 for the state-of-the-art directed fuzzing. Excitingly, with our generated PoCs, Linux kernel maintainers confirmed 69.4% of them, much higher than 9.3% from Sashiko where only textual explanations are included, making AI reviews more actionable in practice.


### arXiv 2611.34130 - WorldPhy: Benchmarking Fine-Grained Physical Plausibility of World Models in Robotics

- categories: cs.RO, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:30:48Z
- authors: Ziyuan Chu, Mingzhi Zheng, Kaiyue Zhang, Jinan Zhang, Linchao Zhu
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34130

World models are increasingly used in robotics to improve the performance of embodied models. Compared with general-purpose video generation, robotics places more stringent requirements on world models. Generated videos must satisfy fine-grained physical constraints on robot motion and object interactions. However, existing world model benchmarks can overlook robotics specific physical details and struggle to assess them accurately. To address this problem, we introduce WorldPhy, a world model benchmark for accurate, fine-grained evaluation of physical plausibility in robotics. Our benchmark contains 300 tasks, each consisting of an initial-frame image and a task instruction, covering diverse robotics scenes, task categories, viewpoints, and robot configurations. We further collect over 12K high-quality human annotations, and use them to train WorldPhyReward to accurately provide scores and reasons across six fine-grained physical dimensions and an instruction-following dimension. Comparisons with general VLMs and existing reward models show that WorldPhyReward better aligns with human ratings. We system- atically evaluate 13 frontier open and closed-source world models. The results reveal that current world models struggle to consistently maintain fine-grained physical plausibility across different robotics scenarios. Our code is available at https://github.com/chuziyuan139/ WorldPhy.


### arXiv 2611.34128 - FoundSurface: Feedforward Ray-Based 3D Scene Surface Reconstruction from Unposed Images

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CG, cs.GR, cs.RO
- published: 2026-11-25T10:30:24Z
- authors: Shenxing Wei, Jinxi Li, Zihui Zhang, Ajay Kumar, Wei Wang, Bo Yang
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34128

Accurate 3D surface reconstruction from unposed images remains a fundamental challenge in computer vision. Recent feed-forward pointmap methods eliminate the need for camera poses but inherently produce discrete, sparse representations. In this paper, we present a generalizable pipeline that directly recovers continuous, high-fidelity 3D surfaces from unposed images by seamlessly integrating pointmap- and ray-based representations. Our approach first extracts explicit geometric priors using a pre-trained pointmap model. A query-view-centric selection module then identifies geometrically relevant reference views via a robust surfel voting mechanism. Next, a cross-attention regressor bridges target query rays with reference features to estimate a continuous coarse surface. Lastly, a raylet-based module aggregates local 3D features to carve out high-frequency details and analytical normals. Trained as a single foundation model on 9 diverse datasets, our method demonstrates exceptional zero-shot generalization on 6 unseen datasets, clearly surpassing state-of-the-art baselines in geometric accuracy and detail preservation.


### arXiv 2611.34126 - Out of Sight, Not Out of Clues: Can Video LLMs Guess from Partial Evidence?

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CL
- published: 2026-11-25T10:30:08Z
- authors: Luoxi Zhang, Jiawei Liu, Yanlin Luo, Peiyang He, Junlin Zhu, Chun To Choi
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34126

In real-world applications like egocentric recording, visual accessibility, and surveillance review, visual evidence for Video Large Language Models (Video LLMs) is often only partially captured. A reliable model should match its response to the evidence: answering when sufficient, guessing from visible clues when partial, and abstaining when absent. Yet existing benchmarks enforce a binary answerable-or-unanswerable split, leaving partial evidence unmeasured and obscuring whether failures arise from missing evidence or misjudging sufficiency. We introduce ClueProbe, a diagnostic benchmark to (1) separate perceiving evidence from judging sufficiency, and (2) validate our diagnosis via targeted fine-tuning. ClueProbe comprises 1,721 human-annotated questions across 598 natural videos, labeled Answerable, Guessable, or Unanswerable, alongside controlled perturbations altering wording or visual input. Evaluating 10 open-source and proprietary Video LLMs reveals that failures stem from judgment rather than perception: models perceive the evidence yet lack graded sufficiency awareness, answering or refusing outright while rarely guessing from partial clues. Targeted fine-tuning shows that this reflects missing supervision rather than insufficient capacity: supervising the guess level lifts appropriate guessing from 1.6% to 40.4% without extra false refusals, whereas conventional refusal tuning rejects 92% of guessable questions. Data and code will be released at https://github.com/suat-llm/ClueProbe.


### arXiv 2611.34122 - MUSE: Multi-Task Uncertainty-Aware Self-Supervised Encoder for mmWave ISAC

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:23:56Z
- authors: Nabeel Nisar Bhat, Jalees Nehvi, Rafael Berkvens, Jeroen Famaey
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34122

Integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) allows a wireless system to use the same waveform for communication and environment sensing. At mmWave frequency bands, ISAC enables diverse human sensing tasks, such as localization, gesture recognition, and pose estimation, with high precision. However, each task requires training a dedicated model, typically from scratch on a task-specific dataset, with no mechanism to flag unreliable predictions. This hurts the scalability, transferability, and reliability of mmWave ISAC models. In this work, we propose MUSE (Multi-task Uncertainty-aware Self-supervised Encoder), a unified framework for mmWave human sensing. MUSE leverages a self-supervised transformer model with per-dataset tokenization and is trained via masked autoencoding across five datasets. During fine-tuning, MUSE uses evidential classification heads that enable uncertainty quantification. MUSE replaces N separate encoders with a single shared transformer encoder, an N-times backbone parameter reduction, while achieving a 9.7 percentage points improvement in classification performance compared to the task-specific model, showing that learning jointly across tasks improves per-task generalization. Across four classification tasks, MUSE produces reliable uncertainty estimates that enable selective prediction, improving accuracy by abstaining on the least confident 20% of predictions. We further improve the reliability of the model's confidence estimates using temperature scaling and conformal prediction. Finally, MUSE's uncertainty signal remains reliable even under cross-user deployment, assigning higher uncertainty to wrong predictions than to correct ones 78% of the time, and recovering 9.2 percentage points of accuracy through selective prediction, with no additional retraining on the unseen user.


### arXiv 2611.34121 - Unstable Action Boundaries in Lethal Drone Agents

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:23:38Z
- authors: Jacques Dumora, André-Louis Rochet, Simon Roburin
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34121

Tool-using large language model (LLM) agents raise safety risks that are not captured by text-only refusal behavior, especially when tools can trigger high-impact actions. This concern is salient in military settings, where autonomous weapons raise the possibility of delegating force decisions to software agents. In this work, we study whether a drone agent's action boundary is stable under changes to the target descriptor. We evaluate recent and popular LLMs in a simulated perception-action pipeline over real-world drone imagery, where models can localize requested targets and invoke a simulated lethal tool. We find two concerning failure modes. First, some LLM agents invoke the lethal tool for ordinary generic descriptors such as \emph{people}, requiring no jailbreak at all. Second, an agent that initially appears conservative under protected descriptors can become unsafe under a simple semantic target shift.


### arXiv 2611.34112 - Not Every Input Needs Everything: Unifying Expert Gating, Model Cascades, and Agent Orchestration

- categories: cs.AI, cs.LG, cs.MA
- published: 2026-11-25T10:15:28Z
- authors: Dat Tat Mai, Hung Tran Quy, Thai Viet Pham, James Jin Kang
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34112

Executing monolithic neural weights, frontier foundation models, or entire multi-agent workflows uniformly on every input incurs substantial computational, latency, and monetary overhead. Conditional computation mitigates this cost by deciding dynamically which internal experts, model endpoints, or specialized agents to invoke per request. Yet token-level gating in sparse neural architectures, query-level model cascades, and task-level multi-agent coordination have largely progressed in disconnected research silos, obscuring recurring system failures and transferable fixes. In this systematic review, we synthesize 2,289 studies published between 2017 and September 2026 (selected from 13,884 unique records) across 20 methodological families under a unified two-step inspect-then-allocate framework. Discrete hard selection accounts for 70%, 83%, and 91% of token-, query-, and task-scale literature respectively, and computational overhead appears as a top operational bottleneck across every layer. Engineering safeguards remain sharply asymmetric: intra-layer gating prioritizes load balancing while rarely incorporating uncertainty estimation, whereas model selection and agent orchestration rely on confidence thresholds and budget rules while confronting miscalibration and cascading errors. At the same time, publication volume is accelerating fastest in agentic task delegation, where routing mechanisms are least often isolated experimentally and methodological bias risk is highest.


### arXiv 2611.34106 - SysBio-SR: Benchmarking Symbolic Regression for Trajectory Fidelity and Mechanism Recovery in Systems Biology

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:12:20Z
- authors: Heng Rao, Dan Gao, Benjamin J Hoffman, Yu Gu, Zhenghao Liu, Ge Yu
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34106

Symbolic regression seeks to discover interpretable governing equations for dynamical systems from data. In systems biology, however, accurately reproducing system trajectories does not necessarily imply recovery of the underlying reaction mechanisms. Existing benchmarks evaluate numerical accuracy, expression complexity, or equation-level similarity, but not agreement between recovered reactions and reference mechanisms in biological systems. We introduce SysBio-SR, a benchmark of 198 literature-based biological ODE systems with reaction annotations spanning mass action, Michaelis--Menten, Hill, and complex kinetics. Trajectory fidelity is evaluated across all systems under in-distribution reconstruction and out-of-distribution (OOD) extrapolation, while mechanism recovery is assessed on the subset of 161 systems. We evaluate trajectory fidelity across 11 symbolic-discovery methods and assess mechanism recovery for three reaction-based methods. Joint analysis across systems highlights how severely trajectory-based evaluation overestimates true mechanism recovery. Motivated by these findings, we develop KineSelect, a framework that searches candidate reaction topologies under biologically grounded kinetic laws to select optimal mechanisms that balance trajectory fidelity with structural parsimony. Compared with the strongest baseline, KineSelect improves aggregate OOD extrapolation \(R^2\) by 19.2\% and overall reaction F1 score by 14.3\%.


### arXiv 2611.34104 - Do Machine Learning Ensembles Beat Published Quotas? Target Parameterization, Censored Baselines, and Conformal Intervals Under Admissions Reform

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:10:06Z
- authors: Dat Tat Mai, James Jin Kang
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34104

Public admission capacity caps published prior to matriculation are widely used by ministries and institutional analysts as default intake projections in centralized postsecondary clearinghouses. Using an audited 2022 to 2026 Vietnamese undergraduate panel (26,895 program-year records across 309 universities) linked to strictly lagged t-1 occupational vacancy signals, this paper evaluates machine learning, multilevel, and censored econometric estimators against published quotas across 5,385 continuing 2024 programs (five-fold university-grouped cross-validation) and 3,348 matched (2,179 grain-consistent) 2025 programs strictly out of time. Because 62.8% of 2024 programs enroll their exact published quota, unanchored log-growth ensembles degrade accuracy, while the worst 5% generate half of total quota error via unfilled "phantom capacity." Controlled target ablations, including a single-factor test under widened non-binding clipping, show that parameterizing the log fill ratio ln(D_it / S_it) instead of log growth reduces 2024 MAE by 21.5% (14.86 to 11.67), aligns ML with Tobit, Cragg, REML HLM, and capped L1 baselines, and lifts combined tuition and institutional-tier TreeSHAP attribution from 2.5% to 45.8%. Hurdle stacking attains the lowest full-census 2024 MAE (H-Hard 11.29; H-Soft 11.32 vs. 12.35, p = 0.022, clearing 10% not 5% FDR; fold-overlap p = 0.134), whereas continuous QA-Stack outperforms H-Soft on mixed-reporting campuses, Tobit (median) ties H-Soft across both 90% MCS sets, and interior-only Cragg-ML lowers RMSE to 28.1. Under Vietnam's 2025 unified-clearing reform, exact filling falls to 10.4%, H-Soft and capped L1 tie quotas (p = 0.979), and 90% cluster-weighted conformal coverage drops from 89.3% to 74.6%. Across 257 reporting campuses, reconciled 2026 national totals (627,114 to 629,256) lie squarely within 604,533 to 662,044.


### arXiv 2611.34101 - AlignPre: Skill-Aligned Post-Training for Latent-Free Generative Precipitation Nowcasting

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:08:09Z
- authors: Demin Yu, Kenghong Lin, Xutao Li
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34101

Precipitation nowcasting requires accurate short-term predictions under rapidly evolving spatiotemporal dynamics. Generative diffusion models can produce detailed and competitive radar forecasts by modeling conditional distributions. However, most existing approaches rely on separately trained components, such as deterministic branches or latent compression modules. More fundamentally, distribution matching alone fails to explicitly optimize operational forecast skill. We propose AlignPre, a streamlined pixel-space nowcasting framework that bridges this gap via skill-aligned post-training (SAPT). AlignPre employs an end-to-end flow-matching forecaster with a hierarchical spatiotemporal architecture for distribution matching and direct radar-field synthesis, without latent compression. During post-training, SAPT exploits the analytic structure of forecast verification scores to construct a differentiable objective. By backpropagating gradients of this objective from radar fields to the generator, SAPT turns forecast verification into direct training feedback. Across three real-world benchmarks spanning different forecast horizons, AlignPre consistently surpasses state-of-the-art deterministic and generative baselines in forecast skill, demonstrating that aligning generative modeling with domain-specific verification provides an effective paradigm for meteorological forecasting.


### arXiv 2611.34099 - Will AI Replace Juniors? How Task Complexity Reduces Automation Risk

- categories: econ.GN, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:05:56Z
- authors: Vincent Rost, Fabian Stephany
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34099

How does task complexity shape firms' willingness to substitute entry-level labour with artificial intelligence (AI) tools? We study this question using a pre-registered survey experiment with managers from 1,435 German firms in the information economy and manufacturing industry. Managers are randomly assigned to consider a long lasting need for additional capacity involving either well-structured and standardised tasks, or more complex tasks that require professional judgement, contextual knowledge and independent problem-solving. Relative to standardised tasks, complex tasks reduce willingness to use AI rather than hire an entry-level employee by 0.54 points on a five-point scale and increase the importance attached to human oversight by 0.15 points. Further, pre-treatment responses indicate that managers expect the availability of AI applications to reduce hiring of both entry-level employees and experienced workers, but significantly more strongly for entry-level workers.


### arXiv 2611.34098 - Whose Side Are You On? A Framework and Benchmark for Assessing LLM Political Stances through Politicians Social Media Posts

- categories: cs.SI, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T10:05:40Z
- authors: Mohamed Osman, Mucahid Kutlu, Tamer Elsayed, Saban Kardas
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34098

Large language models (LLMs) increasingly participate in political discussions, making it important to understand which positions they support or oppose. We propose a framework for assessing LLMs' political stances through simulated social media interactions. The framework uses politicians' posts and multiple prompting methods to identify consistently expressed stances. Using this framework, we construct WorldPOLL, comprising 4,600 political posts from X/Twitter collected from approximately 200 politicians representing ruling and opposition blocs across 23 countries. Our quantitative analysis shows how support and opposition vary across models, countries, and political directions, while our qualitative analysis identifies the political views and policies models support or oppose. Together, these analyses reveal shared positions across models and differences across national contexts. We also examine whether models express consistent stances across prompting methods, investigate the reasons for disagreement, and assess the effects of model size and instruction tuning in stance inconsistency. WorldPOLL offers a practical way to measure LLM political tendencies, identify the views behind them, and assess their consistency in real-world political discussions.


### arXiv 2611.34079 - WorldBridge: Bridging Scattered Views for Coherent 3D World Generation

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T09:48:50Z
- authors: Weilong Yan, Jingyu Hu, Hongli Xu, Yifan Zhan, Beibei Lin, Anbang Wang
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34079

Generating coherent and explorable 3D worlds from scattered images with little or no visual overlap is a fundamental and long-standing challenge in computer vision, which existing geometry-aware methods fail to address. Meanwhile, existing image- and video-based methods may generate unseen regions but produce inconsistent geometry across views. To bridge this gap, we present WorldBridge, a framework for coherent and controllable 3D world generation under scattered images with little or no visual overlap. WorldBridge encodes scattered references into a global scene context to guide geometry and appearance generation across views. Our temporal and spatial reference attention mechanism further enhances the fidelity of temporal references along the target trajectory and supports flexible spatial references outside it. A plug-and-play latent-reference consistency strategy improves reference fidelity through reprojection-guided test-time optimization. We also introduce the WorldBridge benchmark suite to evaluate 3D world generation from scattered, low-overlap references, which is rarely explored by current benchmarks. Both qualitative and quantitative results demonstrate that our method enables controllable and coherent 3D world generation, surpassing the existing state-of-the-art solutions. Our project page is available at https://davidyan2001.github.io/WorldBridge/.


### arXiv 2611.34075 - Hijacking 3D Generation: Targeted Adversarial Attacks against Image-to-3D Models via Prefix Trajectory Alignment

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T09:45:09Z
- authors: Yilong Yang, Jiale Teng, Yule Liu, Yang Liu, Xinlei He, Zhuo Ma
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34075

Online services that deploy Image-to-3D models enable users to generate high-quality 3D assets from a single image. However, the generated 3D geometry can violate content policies and cause broader security risks. To prevent such content, these services typically use policy-restricted inspectors to examine uploaded images before generation. Since the inspector examines only the visible content of the input, a visually benign image can still induce the generation of policy-restricted 3D geometry. However, turning this vulnerability into an effective attack is non-trivial in the black-box setting: global backpropagation through the long DiT generation trajectory is difficult to fit, while different image preprocessing pipelines across services can substantially reduce transferability. In this paper, we present PTA3D, a targeted adversarial attack against Image-to-3D models that addresses these challenges through Prefix Trajectory Alignment (PTA) and Preprocessing Aware Optimization (PAO). PTA progressively aligns the prefix DiT outputs of the adversarial trajectory with those of the target trajectory, where the core geometric structure is formed, allowing the final geometry to be controlled without optimizing through the complete generation trajectory. PAO exposes adversarial optimization to diverse image transformations rather than a single preprocessing pipeline, reducing sensitivity to the mismatch between surrogate and target services. We establish a prefix-to-output bound and a preprocessing-aware transfer bound to theoretically characterize the effectiveness of the two designs. Experiments on three open-source Image-to-3D models and three online services show that PTA3D achieves 41.5%-88.5% ASR on online services and 90.8%-94.6% ASR in white box settings, substantially outperforming existing attacks.


### arXiv 2611.34068 - Budget, Readout or Reasoning? Where Small Reasoning Models Lose Value in Auction Bidding

- categories: cs.AI, cs.CL, econ.GN
- published: 2026-11-25T09:40:08Z
- authors: Mahbod Khaleti, Seyed Reza Tavakoli, Baran Hoseini, Alireza Noori, Amir Amini, Koorosh Aslansefat
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34068

Ad platforms and marketplaces show bidders a bid landscape, the estimated chance of winning at each candidate bid, and increasingly let agents built on reasoning language models choose the bid. We give six open reasoning models of 4B to 14B parameters such landscapes, with win curves from eBay closing prices and from a simulated advertising market (AuctionNet), and score their bids on held-out auctions against an exact calculator. Because the agent is handed the estimates, the lost value can be split by cause. Two failures belong to the models. They pass over the best option even when the values written in their own trace already rank it first, and their wrong answers lean toward the highest bid on the menu. The lean is about the bid's size, not its place in the list, and it survives sampling. At 768 forced tokens on eBay, under pay-your-bid pricing, the wrong answers cost bidders 7-17% of the surplus the calculator's bids earn. Arithmetic errors are rare. Two further losses come from operator choices. A token cap cuts traces short: allowed to stop on their own within 4,096 tokens, five models recover 91-100% of the calculator's lead over a model that does no reasoning. Asking for the answer while the reasoning block is still open misreads it: closing the block first lifts the weakest model from 42% to 84% on eBay and from 24% to 81% on AuctionNet. Finally, an audit that flags bids disagreeing with the trace's own numbers, and overrides them with the calculator's bid, raises the share of the calculator's lead recovered at 768 forced tokens from 42-85% to 84-100% on eBay under nested cross-validation, and the same reader settings transfer from eBay to AuctionNet.


### arXiv 2611.34036 - Few-Shot Adaptation of Vision-Language Models via Agentic Model Editing

- categories: cs.AI, cs.LG
- published: 2026-11-25T09:18:22Z
- authors: Yusheng Zhao, Xiao Luo, Zhiping Xiao, Ming Zhang
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34036

Few-shot adaptation transfers pretrained Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to new domains using a small set of labeled images. Most methods fit prompts, representations, or classifiers within an adaptation procedure specified in advance. Yet the visual distinctions needed for recognition can differ substantially across domains, motivating the question: Can an LLM agent use few-shot examples and model observations to design the adaptation procedure itself? We propose Agentic Model Editing (AME), an agentic framework that jointly designs input transformations, encoder edits, feature computation, and classifier fitting as an executable, domain-customized adaptation program. The agent inspects images, class semantics, model parameters, intermediate representations, and activations to formulate hypotheses about the target task. It implements and tests candidate programs on the support set, with aggregate validation accuracy guiding refinement and selection. Each program fits a complete domain model that is fixed before testing. The agent and its tools are shared across domains, while each domain receives its own adaptation program. Across eleven datasets with CLIP ViT-B/16, AME achieves the highest average accuracies of 81.2% without back-propagation and 81.9% with back-propagation among the compared methods. Further experiments demonstrate applicability across VLM backbones and LLM agents.


### arXiv 2611.34035 - Post-Training Policy Replication for Strategy-Following Multi-Agent Deep Reinforcement Learning across Team Sizes

- categories: cs.MA, cs.AI, cs.LG
- published: 2026-11-25T09:18:04Z
- authors: Yamato Takahagi, Yoshinari Motokawa, Toshiharu Sugawara
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34035

Although multi-agent deep reinforcement learning enables coordinated behavior, aligning it with managerial intent remains difficult due to complex agent interactions. Although the strategy-following distributed attentional actor architecture (sfDA7-X) improves controllability via regional instructions, instruction combinations scale exponentially with team and instruction sizes, leading to prohibitive training costs and poor transferability. To reduce this computational cost, we introduce a team-size-independent observation architecture and reconstruct collective behavior by replicating a single trained policy across agents in teams of different sizes, thereby eliminating the need to train from scratch. Additionally, we evaluate warm-start retraining by initializing policy networks with a pre-trained policy to significantly lower computational overhead. In an object collection game, a policy trained in a four-agent team is deployed to teams of three to eight agents. Experiments demonstrate that training with the proposed aggregated observation matrix achieves performance comparable to that of the baseline method, sfDA7-X. Furthermore, policy replication preserves performance without substantial degradation in three- and five-agent teams, and warm-start retraining outperforms training from scratch while reducing required training episodes by up to 90%. Deployments to six- and eight-agent teams further confirm effective spatial region allocation following given instructions. These results demonstrate that the proposed method enables zero-shot collective behavior reconstruction via policy replication.


### arXiv 2611.34032 - Counter-DECEIVR: Counterfactual Explanations for Deepfake Detectors using Detector-Guided Rectified Flow Inversion

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T09:16:25Z
- authors: Mariano V. Ntrougkas, Vasileios Mezaris, Ioannis Patras
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34032

Deep-learning-generated media are increasingly passed off as real, driving an arms race between image generators and deepfake detectors. Trust in the latter is limited by their often black-box nature; and, while region-based explanations can sometimes be extracted, these become less useful as generation quality improves. Counterfactual examples, which show the minimal change that flips a detector's decision, reveal what evidence drives a verdict, helping analysts decide whether to trust it. This work introduces Counter-DECEIVR (DEtector-guided reCtifiEd flow InVeRsion for counterfactuals), a novel training-free framework that generates counterfactual examples. Counter-DECEIVR steers latents in the velocity field of a rectified flow model using gradients from off-the-shelf deepfake detectors, without training a generator or adapting the detector; unlike prior methods, it requires no per-instance or per-detector fitting and no diffusion training on the target data. It also leverages the detector's explanation maps to concentrate editing to salient regions, turning low-level feature importance into localised counterfactual edits. A single parameter controls the strength of the edits. Across two deepfake detectors and two widely used forensics datasets, Counter-DECEIVR outperforms prior counterfactual generation methods, including training-based ones. Code will be made available upon acceptance.


### arXiv 2611.34025 - Incorporating the Knowledge Distillation to Improve the EfficientNet Transfer Learning Capability

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T09:12:38Z
- authors: Tjeng Wawan Cenggoro
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34025

Due to the Deep Learning requirement for a large training dataset, Transfer Learning has become a central method in the field of Computer Vision, which heavily used Deep Learning. Since the adoption of transfer learning in the field, the performance of the models in computer vision is significantly upgraded. The common transfer learning practice in computer vision research is to use state-of-the-art architectures in the ImageNet dataset as the backbone network. The best performing architecture in ImageNet is currently held by EfficientNet, which is a set of architectures that were progressively grown from a baseline architecture. By observing the EfficientNet development approach, it is natural to hypothesize that the smaller EfficientNet architecture is capable to contain the knowledge of the larger architecture. This hypothesis comes from the fact that the larger EfficientNet architecture was grown from the smaller architecture. Therefore, in this study, we experimented if it is beneficial to transfer the knowledge from the larger architecture to the smaller architecture of the EfficientNet. To achieve this goal, we proposed a transfer learning method that uses Knowledge Distillation as the knowledge transfer mechanism from the larger to smaller architecture. We found that the proposed method is able to upgrade the performance of each of the EfficientNet architecture. Several smaller architectures are even able to outperform the larger architecture that was trained using the standard transfer learning method.


### arXiv 2611.34019 - Assessing the Physical Interpretability of AlphaEarth Foundation Embeddings Through Multi-Sensor Satellite Time-Series Benchmarking for Agricultural Mapping

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T09:07:05Z
- authors: El Houcine El Moussaoui, Chouaib El Hachimi, Saïd Khabba, Salwa Belaqziz, Bouchra Ait Hssaine, Abderrahman Lahrouni
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34019

Accurate and dynamic maps of agricultural LULC are important for agricultural water resource management, particularly in areas where water scarcity is prevalent, such as Morocco. Here, we evaluate the utility of AlphaEarth Foundation Model (AEF) embeddings for agricultural LULC and irrigation-type mapping in Morocco. AEF embeddings were compared to single-date and time-series remote-sensing features using three machine-learning classifiers: XGBoost, CatBoost, and TabPFN. We also evaluated whether AEF embeddings can: (a) provide an efficient and transferable alternative to conventional direct use of Earth observation products and (b) reconstruct biophysical information. Results showed that multi-temporal feature time series achieved the highest performance, with accuracies of 0.93 for agricultural LULC and 0.87 for irrigation-type mapping when using TabPFN. However, AEF embeddings yielded comparable results, achieving accuracies of 0.91 for agricultural LULC and 0.85 for irrigation-type mapping, while reducing the need for extensive data processing and manual feature engineering typically required for satellite products. Spatial LULC mapping using TabPFN achieved comparable overall accuracies (0.85-0.86) across the three feature configurations, but revealed substantial differences in mapped tree cover, ranging from 14.57% for surface reflectance to 29.79% for AFE. Physical probing revealed that AEF embeddings strongly encode spectral indices (A30-SWIR: rho=0.58-0.67), (A38-NDMI: rho=-0.72), vegetation (A49-NDVI: rho = 0.72-0.73), and SAR structure (A25-VH: rho = 0.71). Future research should focus on enhancing the interpretability of embedding-based approaches by incorporating physical knowledge to develop more transparent and trustworthy AI systems for agricultural applications.


### arXiv 2611.34016 - Evaluating Ukrainian Grammatical Competence in Language Models: Probability Versus Prompting

- categories: cs.CL, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T09:05:09Z
- authors: Dmytro Babarytskyi, Oleksandr Babarytskyi, Vadym Savanevych
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34016

Organisations deploying large language models in Ukrainian-language information systems need to know whether a model produces normative Ukrainian. This paper establishes a protocol for measuring that reliably and applies it to 19 models. The instrument is a released set of 1,444 Ukrainian minimal pairs covering the vocative case, numeral-noun and adjective agreement, verb government and calques from russian, with subject-verb agreement as a control; a quarter of the set was checked against the answer key in two independent native-speaker ratings, which agreed at Cohen's $κ$ = 0.963. Every pair is scored twice: by comparing the probabilities a model assigns to the two sentences, and by asking the model directly, with the sentences presented in both orders so that answer-position effects are measured instead of being absorbed into the score. Three results follow. Order consistency (the share of pairs answered identically when the sentences are exchanged) falls below 0.05 for ten of the nineteen models, and what separates the usable models is instruction tuning, not scale. The vocative case is systematically inverted: 0.153 accuracy against 0.951 on the control, with models preferring the non-normative nominative form of address. Ukrainian adaptation substantially reduces this deficit, measured against the exact checkpoints two adapted models were trained from.


### arXiv 2611.34015 - Residual Cross-Model Narrative Convergence under Shared Prompts: Auditing Prompt Controls and Narrative Measurement

- categories: cs.CL, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T09:03:56Z
- authors: Mehdy Sedaghat Payam
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34015

Stories created by LLMs based on the same prompts can be narratively similar to one another. This similarity may reflect constraints imposed by the prompts as well as the models' writing tendencies. The current research analyzes 51,240 stories created by five models in the StoryScope corpus based on 10,248 common prompts. It uses 213 narrative features in seven dimensions to compare cross-model distances between stories generated from the same prompts with distances between stories generated from different but semantically matched prompts. To assess the relationship between narrative features and prompt content, a semantic relevance mask was evaluated against 420 author-annotated prompt-feature judgments (F1 = .813; near-threshold accuracy = .621). The equally weighted mean Residual Convergence Index (RCI) was .409 without masking and .406 with masking. These findings indicate measurable cross-model narrative convergence relative to a semantically matched-prompt baseline, with little change following semantic relevance masking.


### arXiv 2611.34012 - CARROT: Competitive Adaptive Graph Clustering via Semi-Relaxed Optimal Transport

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T08:59:46Z
- authors: Ching Fan Chiang, Jia-Yu Pan, Wen-Chieh Lin
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34012

Deep graph clustering (DGC) has emerged as a prominent paradigm for unsupervised attributed graph clustering. However, existing methods often struggle with two practical challenges encountered in real-world applications: the number of clusters $K$ is unknown a priori, and cluster sizes are frequently highly imbalanced. Methods relying on a pre-specified $K$ may fail to recover the underlying clustering structure when $K$ is misspecified, while many approaches implicitly favor balanced partitions, hindering the discovery of minority clusters. We propose CARROT, a Competitive Adaptive gRaph clustering framework via semi-Relaxed Optimal Transport, which introduces semi-relaxed optimal transport into deep graph clustering to jointly address both challenges. CARROT first pre-trains node embeddings to establish a meaningful embedding space, and then jointly optimizes embeddings and cluster assignments through an Adaptive Clustering Mechanism. The mechanism combines semi-relaxed optimal transport to adapt cluster capacities to the underlying data, a competition loss to concentrate mass onto well-supported cluster prototypes, thereby starving degenerate ones, and hard pruning to progressively eliminate them. Extensive experiments on five public benchmarks demonstrate that CARROT achieves state-of-the-art NMI and ARI, accurately recovers the number of clusters without supervision, and faithfully preserves size-imbalanced community structures.


### arXiv 2611.34011 - Honest but Wrong: Formalizing Retraction in the Ethics of Speech Acts

- categories: cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T08:58:14Z
- authors: Benjamin Icard, Gauvain Bourgne, Jeanne Bonnaventure, Jean-Gabriel Ganascia
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34011

A speaker who asserts honestly may later discover that its assertion was false. Such erroneous truths are not lies, yet they raise the question of whether, and on which moral grounds, the speaker must retract them. Using the FIPA-ACL performative disconfirm, we extend a belief-based framework that evaluates speech acts under deontologism and consequentialism to the case of retraction. We prove that the honesty duty of deontologism, which bears on utterances when they are made, never requires retracting an erroneous truth, and add a persistent one, which forbids letting the addressee believe what the speaker no longer believes. On a legal-advice case implemented in Answer Set Programming, we show that deontologism and consequentialism both require the retraction, but that consequentialism no longer does once it comes too late to prevent harm.


### arXiv 2611.34008 - DosePET: An Open-Source Dataset with 2100 Radiation Dose Maps for PET/CT

- categories: physics.med-ph, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T08:55:37Z
- authors: Eleftherios Tzanis, Antonios Tzortzakakis, Michail E. Klontzas
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.34008

Large collections of three-dimensional radiation dose maps linked to patient anatomy are needed to develop artificial intelligence (AI) models for personalized internal dosimetry in nuclear medicine, but access to such data remains limited. Here we present DosePET, an open-source dataset of 2,100 patient-specific absorbed-dose maps generated by Monte Carlo simulation of publicly available PET/CT examinations: 1,503 [18F]FDG examinations from ENHANCE.PET and 369 [18F]PSMA-1007 and 228 [68Ga]Ga-PSMA-11 examinations from PSMA-PET-CT-Lesions, acquired at five institutions with seven scanner models. For each examination, the CT was resampled to the native PET grid to build a voxelized patient phantom, the quantitative PET image defined the radionuclide source, and radiation transport was simulated with GATE version 9.4. Organ and tissue masks were generated from the CT with TotalSegmentator. Each study folder contains a NIfTI dose map integrated from scan time to infinity, 117 binary organ/tissue masks, a report of regional dosimetric quantities, including mean absorbed doses, dose conversion factors and scan-time dose rates, and simulation metadata. The total processing time was 1,542.4 hours. Python tools support selective downloading and conversion to scan-time dose-rate or dose-per-decay maps, and an agent skill guides pairing with the source PET/CT images. DosePET is publicly available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/eltzanis/DosePET.


### arXiv 2611.33993 - Aegis-VLA: Reliability-Gated Action Refinement via a Trajectory World Model for Dynamic Object Manipulation

- categories: cs.RO, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T08:46:50Z
- authors: Lennox Wei, Jiaqi Yao, Shixin Sun, Yuanzheng Zhang, Jianhua Han, Meng Tian
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33993

Dynamic object manipulation requires maintaining spatial and temporal alignment with targets that continue to move during inference and execution. Although future prediction can inform action correction, an accurate forecast does not establish whether modifying an existing action would be beneficial. We present Aegis-VLA, a world-model-guided vision-language-action (VLA) framework that generates predictive action corrections and separately determines whether and how strongly to apply them. A Trajectory World Model combines kinematic extrapolation with learned residual dynamics to predict object motion and estimate positional uncertainty. Discrepancy-Guided Action Refinement uses differences between history-only and base-action-conditioned end-effector forecasts to propose bounded arm corrections. A Reliability Gate learns when to admit corrections based on demonstration-relative improvements over base actions, and regulates their application during inference using predictive uncertainty and anticipated object--end-effector proximity. Rejected corrections preserve the corresponding base actions. Aegis-VLA introduces only approximately 2.45M trainable parameters. On the simulated Dynamic Object Manipulation (DOM) benchmark, Aegis-VLA achieves 56.72\% success over 1,800 evaluation trials, exceeding our reproduced DynamicVLA baseline of 45.44\% by 11.28 percentage points.


### arXiv 2611.33985 - MolGPAO: Molecular Generative Pre-trained Autoregressive Octree with Speculative Decoding and Sub-Millisecond Seam Healing for 3D Molecules

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI, q-bio.BM
- published: 2026-11-25T08:41:30Z
- authors: Shangqian Chen, Guojiang Zhao, Zheng Cheng, Jiankun Wang, Hongshuai Wang, Zhifeng Gao
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33985

Autoregressive generation over adaptive 3D octrees (Uni-3DAR) offers an intrinsic $O(N \log N)$ sparsity advantage over dense $O(N^2)$ equivariant diffusion and flow models on large drug-like molecules, yet lags in geometric equilibrium, closed-shell valence stability, and property fidelity due to Morton-seam coordinate jumps, leaf-heavy compute with root-count trapping, and target-shortcut collapse in internal critics. We introduce MolGPAO (Molecular Generative Pre-trained Autoregressive Octree), a standalone 3D octree architecture and single-output speculative tree-decoding engine using zero external surrogates, zero reinforcement learning, and zero inference-time MLIP relaxation. MolGPAO unifies: (1) non-trapping 6-D ScaleFreeBudgetEmbedding with L5-to-L1 covalent packing priors; (2) a FlashAttention-2 pause-and-gather speculative octree funnel ($m_{s1} \to m_{s2} \to 1$, $M_{\mathrm{out}} = 1$); (3) an anti-shortcut ground-truth TwoStageOctreeProcessCritic with 64-RBF covalent edge gating and $SO(3)$-invariant 12-channel multipole norms (blind QM9 $r = 0.9867$--$0.9989$); (4) a $<0.5$ ms 4-layer $SE(3)$ PostTreeGraphRefiner for Morton-seam healing, expected-XYZ bonded supervision, selective CFG, and zero-heavy-displacement valence reconciliation; and (5) bubble-free stride-2 GPU leaf decoding. Across GEOM-Drugs (0.49 GPU-h), disjoint 50k/50k QM9 (0.43 GPU-h), and PBE0/def2-TZVP-verified OE62 (0.56 GPU-h), MolGPAO lifts drug-scale valid-connected yield 6.78x (12.7% to 86.2%, 99.83% atom stability), runs 7.3x faster (or 1.65x faster at 2.0x yield) than 100-step FlowMol at $N=100$, beats 156 GPU-h PropMolFlow on QM9 HOMO (-12.7%) and dipole (-21.0%) at 362x lower compute, and doubles live DFT hit rate on OE62 (33.3% vs. 16.7%) at 92.8x lower compute.


### arXiv 2611.33973 - Kolpona: An Agentic Framework with Gradient-Free Neuron Programming for RTL Generation

- categories: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.AR
- published: 2026-11-25T08:34:49Z
- authors: Musa Tur Farazi, Md Nasir Uddin, Soumik Bhattacharjee, Tishna Sabrina, Chowdhury Mohammad Rakin Haider
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33973

Open-weight large language models (LLMs) for register-transfer level (RTL) design are usually improved by fine-tuning on curated Verilog corpora, which needs large sets of verified designs and GPU training. We present Kolpona, an agentic framework that improves such models without gradient training. Its first component is neuron programming, a new form of weight-space programming (WSP) that writes single neurons in closed form into the weights to remove failure habits found on practice tasks, and keeps each edit only when testbench results on fresh tasks favour it. At inference time, tool-using agents propose designs, and a label-free selection stage delivers one design per task from the specification, the candidates and their simulations alone. On Qwen3.5-4B, edits to 16 neurons raise the VerilogEval v2 pass@1 of the model alone from 31.7% to 55.4%, and the full framework reaches 77.6%. On Qwen3.8-27B, Kolpona lifts pass@1 from 73.1% to 94.9% on VerilogEval v2 and from 66.0% to 92.0% on RTLLM v2.1 on a single 32 GB GPU, above every published system in our comparison. The RTL edits also carry over to Python and raise the pass@1 of the 4B model alone from 84.8% to 91.5% on HumanEval+ and from 67.7% to 77.5% on MBPP+.


### arXiv 2611.33972 - Fine-Tuning Without Gradients? Closed-Form, LLM-Written Classifiers on a Frozen Encoder

- categories: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.LG
- published: 2026-11-25T08:34:46Z
- authors: Md Nasir Uddin, Musa Tur Farazi, Soumik Bhattacharjee, Tishna Sabrina, Chowdhury Mohammad Rakin Haider
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33972

Can a classifier computed in closed form on a frozen encoder match gradient fine-tuning? We present closed-form weight-space programming (WSP): a large language model (LLM), used as a design assistant, writes programs that read a frozen masked language model through cloze-style prompt templates at several layers, fit closed-form heads (shrinkage discriminant analysis, kernel ridge regression and the encoder's label-word prior) on labelled data, and select among a pool of candidate heads by cross-fitting. No gradient is computed and no encoder weight changes. We instantiate the framework on RoBERTa-large. On 11 development datasets with 512 to 8,192 training labels, WSP is ahead of full fine-tuning (FT) and LoRA at every label budget (by 0.3 to 0.6 points on average; the registered non-inferiority tests pass) and within 0.5 points of prompt-based fine-tuning (PFT); these datasets also shaped the recipe. With fewer labels its lead over FT and LoRA grows: 1.2 to 3.5 points with 64 and 128 labels, and 3.8 to 5.3 points in the few-shot setting of 16 labels per class, where WSP is again within half a point of PFT on the development datasets. On six held-out datasets, WSP stays ahead of LoRA and within half a point of FT on average, although with six datasets no registered test passes, and PFT is ahead, by 1.30 points with 8,192 labels. WSP never collapses and is deterministic, but it needs one encoder pass per template (17 here). We report all eight recipe versions, every decision that saw test results, and 27 ideas that did not help.


### arXiv 2611.33966 - PatternMiner: Automated Mathematical Discovery Through LLM-Guided Training and Interpretation of Small Models

- categories: cs.AI, math.CO
- published: 2026-11-25T08:30:13Z
- authors: Michael Ruofan Zeng, Junaid Hasan, Xuanyu Yang, Emily Xia Meng, Henry Kvinge
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33966

There are several approaches to AI-driven mathematical discovery. One of these involves the use of large language models (LLMs) to resolve well-specified conjectures via text-based reasoning, while another uses narrow models and interpretability for more open-ended, human-in-the-loop exploration using object-based mathematical data. The latter is limited in its ability to move beyond simple empirical pattern detection while the former is not well-designed for direct, open-ended exploration of large mathematical datasets. In this paper we introduce PatternMiner, a system that combines these two paradigms. In PatternMiner, an LLM agent generates mathematical datasets based on an initial broad question, trains narrow machine learning models on them, uses interpretability techniques to extract patterns from these trained models, and uses these patterns to formulate conjectures. Once an initial conjecture has been formulated, PatternMiner instructs the agent to search for counterexamples using calls to mathematical software. If a set of counterexamples can be found, these are used as feedback for conjecture refinement or another round of training and interpretability. The conjecture progressively evolves through this iterative sequence with the agent allowed to try to prove it at any point. We describe the results of applying PatternMiner to five well-known positivity problems in algebraic combinatorics. PatternMiner proves several new theorems related to Schubert positivity and projection, a character-row evaluation formula, a restricted version of the Kazhdan--Lusztig combinatorial invariance conjecture, and Schur positivity for a family of immanant coefficients. We end by analyzing the most common routes to conjecture generation that are used by PatternMiner in our case studies as well as documenting the most common failure modes.


### arXiv 2611.33964 - Embodied-VGGT: Preserving Reliable Local Geometry for Long-Sequence Reconstruction

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI
- published: 2026-11-25T08:28:45Z
- authors: Yiheng Han, Ziang Chen, Zhuyong Fu, Yiqing Yin, Nan Ma
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33964

Long sequence reconstruction must correct accumulated drift while preserving locally consistent geometry. In robotics and autonomous driving, embodied acquisition provides recurring platform- environment geometry and repeated observations of scene structure and platform motion. We present Embodied-VGGT, which exploits these cues to establish reliable local relationships and selectively preserve them during global refinement. Memory-Augmented Grounded Chunks (MAGC) builds on Scal3R's global context memory and uses relative scale references derived from predicted ground geometry to regularize test-time memory updates, encouraging cross-chunk consistency while limiting changes to reliable local geometry. Superpoint Alignment (SA) and Cross-Loop Optimization (CL) jointly address the robust establishment and preservation of geometric relationships. SA balances ground and non-ground evidence across spatial regions to estimate inter-chunk translation. CL uses geometric and motion consistency across interleaved windows to select coherent chunk groups, each receiving a shared Sim(3) correction that preserves its internal relative transformations during global optimization. Experiments on five public driving benchmarks demonstrate consistent and substantial improvements in long sequence reconstruction. On KITTI Odometry, Embodied-VGGT achieves a mean absolute trajectory error of 7.30 m and a Chamfer distance of 2.67 m, reducing these errors by 46.8% and 23.3% compared with the state-of-the-art baseline under same evaluation protocol. To facilitate reproducibility, we released our code: https://github.com/Ang-Sheep/Embodied-VGGT.


### arXiv 2611.33961 - AI Buckingham: Agentic Discovery of Dimensionless Scaling Laws Across Physical Regimes

- categories: physics.comp-ph, cs.AI, physics.flu-dyn
- published: 2026-11-25T08:24:47Z
- authors: Ao Cheng, Lei Zhang, Guowei He
- query-set: cs.AI
- abs: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.33961

Classical dimensional analysis defines admissible dimensionless representations but does not determine which combinations capture the dominant physical dependence, whether it changes across physical regimes, or when dependent-variable rescaling is required. We present AI Buckingham, an agentic framework for autonomous dimensionless discovery from natural-language descriptions of physical problems and observational data. It couples reasoning and planning by a large language model (LLM) with predefined symbolic and numerical tools. Literature-based knowledge retrieval supports dimensional checking, gradient-based clustering identifies physical regimes, and mutual information provides an objective, reparameterization-invariant criterion for identifying dominant dimensionless groups. Explicit numerical criteria determine the group count and assess simplification. Across ten physical systems spanning fluid dynamics, magnetohydrodynamics, thermal convection and additive manufacturing, AI Buckingham recovers established similarity representations and resolves regime-dependent scalings. For the longstanding challenge of predicting turbulent drag on rough surfaces, it identifies informative reparameterizations of surface statistics that improve equivalent sand-grain roughness estimates for engineering-oriented drag modelling.


### arXiv 2611.33957 - RefMix: Expanding Endpoint Proposals for Diversity-Preserving Reward Fine-Tuning

- categories: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
- published: 2026-11-25T08:18:11Z
